<div class='question question'>

**Questions** $\bold{36}$ **to** $\bold{38}$ **relate to the triangle numbers**.

The Fibonacci numbers are a sequence of numbers formed so that the first two
numbers are $0$ and $1$, and each subsequent number is the sum of the two previous numbers.

So the first eight terms of the sequence are:

$0, 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 13$

What is the next Fibonacci number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by adding together the last two terms. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\maroonC
 {(0 + 0)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
 {(0 + 1)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
 {(1 + 1)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
 {(1 + 2)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
 {(2 + 3)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
 {(3 + 5)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
 {(5 + 8)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC
 {(8 + 13)} }  \\
0, && 1,  &&  1, &&  2, &&  3, &&  5,  &&   8,  &&  13,  && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Missing number}       &= 8 + 13 \\\\
                            &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The next term is calculated by adding together the last two terms. Hence, the sequence would be:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(0 + 0)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(0 + 1)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(1 + 1)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(1 + 2)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(2 + 3)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(3 + 5)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(5 + 8)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(8 + 13)} }  \\
0, && 1,  &&  1, &&  2, &&  3, &&  5,  &&   8,  &&  13,  && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Missing number}       &= 8 + 13 \\\\
                            &= 21
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$21$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>
</li>
<li>

<div class='question question'>

The fourteenth Fibonacci number is $377$ and the fifteenth Fibonacci number is
$610$.

What is the sixteenth Fibonacci number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Sixteenth Fibonacci number}  &= \text {Fourteenth Fibonacci number} + \text {Fifteenth Fibonacci number} \\\\
                                    &= 377 + 610 \\\\
                                    &= 987
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$987$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$

                                    &= 377 + 610 \\\\
                                    &= 987
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$987$
</code></pre>

\dfrac {5} {8},  &&    \dfrac {10} {16},  &&  \dfrac {15} {24},   && \ldots
\end{matrix}

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by adding $3$ to the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(+3)}}  \\
10, && 13, && 16, && \ldots,&& \ldots, && 25, && 28
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}       &= 16 + 3 \\\\
                                  &= 19 \\\\
\text{Second missing number}      &= 19 + 3 \\\\
                                  &= 22
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='answer'>

$19$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

<div class='question question'>

Bob makes a sequence using the following rule: $\text{‘double and subtract 3’}$. If the first number in his sequence is $5$, what will the fifth number in his sequence be?

A: $157$ 

B: $77$ 

C: $19$ 

D: $35$ 

E: $67$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Fifth number}    &= 5 \times (5 \times 2 - 3) \\\\
                        &= 5 \times (10 - 3) \\\\
                        &= 5 \times 7 \\\\
                        &= 35
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$35$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Fifth number}    &= 5 \times (5 \times 2 - 3) \\\\
                        &= 5 \times (10 - 3) \\\\
                        &= 5 \times 7 \\\\
                        &= 35
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>D</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$35$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>
</li>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by adding $6$ to the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC
 { (+6)} }
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
 { (+6)} }
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
 { (+6)} }
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
 { (+6)} }
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
 { (+6)} }  \\
4, && 10, && 16, && 22, && \ldots, && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}     &= 22 + 6 \\\\
                                &= 28 \\\\
\text{Second missing number}    &= 28 + 6 \\\\
                                &= 34
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

The number pattern below is known as Pascal’s Triangle. Each number is the sum of the two numbers directly above it.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Complete the table below.

| ROW                 | TOTAL                 |                       |
|:-----------------:  |:-----------------:    |:-----------------:    |
| $1$                 |  $1$                  |                       |
| $2$                 | $2$                   |        $2$            |
| $3$                 | $4$                   |     $2\times2$        |
| $4$                 |                       | $2\times2\times2$     |
| $5$                 |                       |                       |
| $6$                 |                       |                       |
| $7$                 |                       |                       |

What will be the total in row $10$?

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_g0 rag_prcr question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>11864F13-15AE-442B-A8E6-F4F08A54A7D5</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>reigate-grammar-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-4:1:38</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g0</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prcr</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tsequence
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

What is the $100^\text{th}$ symbol in this endless string of symbols?

$&£=&£=&£=&£=&£=$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

After oberserving the pattern, the first symbol is &, second is f and third is =. This pattern is repeating in a set of 3.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {100} {3} \\\\
&= 33 \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$

As the remainder is $1$, The symbol in the first position is going to repeat in the $100$th position.

The $100$th symbol is $=$ &

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

&

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>After oberserving the pattern, the first symbol is &, second is f and third is =. This pattern is repeating in a set of 3.
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {100} {3} \\\\
&= 33 \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As the remainder is $1$, The symbol in the first position is going to repeat in the $100$th position.
<emptyline>
The $100$th symbol is $=$ &
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>&
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Write down the next two numbers in the sequence:

$14, \quad 10, \quad 6, \quad 2, \quad \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore }, \quad \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore }$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by subtracting $4$ from the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin {matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC
{(-4)}}  \\
14,  &&    10,  &&  6,  &&   2,  &&   \ldots, && \ldots
\end {matrix}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {First missing number}       &= 2 - 4 \\\\
                                   &= -2 \\\\
\text {Second missing number}      &= -2 - 4 \\\\
                                   &= -6
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$-2$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$-6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The next term is calculated by subtracting $4$ from the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:
<emptyline>
$
\begin {matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(-4)}}  \\
14,  &&    10,  &&  6,  &&   2,  &&   \ldots, && \ldots
\end {matrix}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {First missing number}       &= 2 - 4 \\\\
                                   &= -2 \\\\
\text {Second missing number}      &= -2 - 4 \\\\
                                   &= -6
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$-2$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$-6$
</code></pre>
</div>
